### Diffusion Model Quantization: A Review
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05215v1)
- **Authors**: Qian Zeng, Chenggong Hu, Mingli Song, Jie Song
- **Abstract**: Recent success of large text-to-image models has empirically underscored the exceptional performance of diffusion models in generative tasks. To facilitate their efficient deployment on resource-constrained edge devices, model quantization has emerged as a pivotal technique for both compression and acceleration. This survey offers a thorough review of the latest advancements in diffusion model quantization, encapsulating and analyzing the current state of the art in this rapidly advancing domain. First, we provide an overview of the key challenges encountered in the quantization of diffusion models, including those based on U-Net architectures and Diffusion Transformers (DiT). We then present a comprehensive taxonomy of prevalent quantization techniques, engaging in an in-depth discussion of their underlying principles. Subsequently, we perform a meticulous analysis of representative diffusion model quantization schemes from both qualitative and quantitative perspectives. From a quantitative standpoint, we rigorously benchmark a variety of methods using widely recognized datasets, delivering an extensive evaluation of the most recent and impactful research in the field. From a qualitative standpoint, we categorize and synthesize the effects of quantization errors, elucidating these impacts through both visual analysis and trajectory examination. In conclusion, we outline prospective avenues for future research, proposing novel directions for the quantization of generative models in practical applications. The list of related papers, corresponding codes, pre-trained models and comparison results are publicly available at the survey project homepage https://github.com/TaylorJocelyn/Diffusion-Model-Quantization.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and a critical evaluation with a justified score for the paper "Diffusion Model Quantization: A Review."

**Summary:**

This paper presents a comprehensive review of recent advancements in diffusion model quantization, a crucial technique for efficiently deploying these computationally intensive generative models on resource-constrained devices. The authors categorize and analyze various quantization methods tailored for diffusion models, including those based on U-Net and Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architectures.  The review covers both post-training quantization (PTQ) and quantization-aware training (QAT) techniques, examining strategies like calibration sampling, dynamic activation, and error correction. A significant portion of the work involves benchmarking open-source solutions across different image generation tasks and offering qualitative analyses of quantization artifacts such as color bias and blurring. Finally, the authors outline future research directions for the quantization of generative models in practical applications. They provide extensive resources on a linked survey project webpage, including code and pre-trained model information.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary contribution of this paper is as a *survey*. Surveys, by nature, do not present fundamentally *new* methods or theoretical results. However, their novelty lies in their *synthesis* of existing knowledge, *identification* of key trends, and *framing* of future research directions. This paper is, according to the authors, the first dedicated survey on diffusion model quantization, a rapidly evolving field.  The taxonomy presented is relatively well thought out and divides into relevant categories, like U-Net and transformer approaches, PTQ and QAT, and a categorization of solutions to the specific problems of the architectures. The compilation of papers, codes, pre-trained models, and comparison results on a survey website is valuable.

*   **Significance:** Diffusion models are at the forefront of generative AI. Quantizing them is essential for making them practical on edge devices and in other resource-limited scenarios. Therefore, a comprehensive survey is highly relevant and important. The paper brings together fragmented research, allowing researchers to grasp the landscape of existing quantization techniques for diffusion models quickly. By highlighting the key challenges and comparing different approaches, it will enable researchers to build upon the work. The benchmark and qualitative studies offer a practical perspective and provide insights into the trade-offs between different quantization methods.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensiveness:** The survey appears to cover a wide range of relevant papers, including the most recent advancements.
    *   **Clear Taxonomy:** The structured categorization of quantization techniques makes the review easy to navigate.
    *   **Practical Evaluation:** The benchmark experiments and qualitative analysis provide valuable insights into the performance and limitations of different methods.
    *   **Future Directions:** Identifying promising areas for future research helps to guide the field.
    *   **Resource provision:** Creating and making available the links to codes and models is very useful for other researches.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth in Novel Results:** As a survey, it doesn't present novel theoretical insights or introduce fundamentally new quantization techniques. While the organization and categorization are useful, they are somewhat derivative from previous work in quantization.

* **Justification of Score:**
A paper's main strength is how much future researchers are helped and influenced to move to the next advancement. Here, having all the papers, codes and pre-trained model ready for research is extremely helpful. Given that the diffusion model is a hot area, helping research will cause ripple effects. The paper achieves high level of comprehensiveness and clear organization. The limitations are mainly due to the inherent nature of review papers, but the authors do an excellent job of fulfilling the core purposes. The score reflects the paper's value in consolidating the field of diffusion model quantization and guiding further research.

### Normalize Everything: A Preconditioned Magnitude-Preserving Architecture for Diffusion-Based Speech Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05216v1)
- **Authors**: Julius Richter, Danilo de Oliveira, Timo Gerkmann
- **Abstract**: This paper presents a new framework for diffusion-based speech enhancement. Our method employs a Schroedinger bridge to transform the noisy speech distribution into the clean speech distribution. To stabilize and improve training, we employ time-dependent scalings of the inputs and outputs of the network, known as preconditioning. We consider two skip connection configurations, which either include or omit the current process state in the denoiser's output, enabling the network to predict either environmental noise or clean speech. Each approach leads to improved performance on different speech enhancement metrics. To maintain stable magnitude levels and balance during training, we use a magnitude-preserving network architecture that normalizes all activations and network weights to unit length. Additionally, we propose learning the contribution of the noisy input within each network block for effective input conditioning. After training, we apply a method to approximate different exponential moving average (EMA) profiles and investigate their effects on the speech enhancement performance. In contrast to image generation tasks, where longer EMA lengths often enhance mode coverage, we observe that shorter EMA lengths consistently lead to better performance on standard speech enhancement metrics. Code, audio examples, and checkpoints are available online.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Normalize Everything: A Preconditioned Magnitude-Preserving Architecture for Diffusion-Based Speech Enhancement":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel diffusion-based speech enhancement framework built upon a Schrödinger bridge to transform noisy speech into clean speech. The core contributions revolve around stabilizing and improving training through: (1) time-dependent preconditioning (scaling inputs and outputs), (2) exploring two skip connection configurations (environmental noise vs. clean speech prediction as the training target), (3) a magnitude-preserving network architecture (normalizing activations and weights), and (4) learning the noisy input contribution within each network block. The authors also experiment with different exponential moving average (EMA) profiles during inference, observing that shorter EMA lengths perform better in speech enhancement, a finding that contrasts with image generation tasks. They benchmark their approach against existing methods on VoiceBank-DEMAND and EARS-WHAM_v2 datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strengths:** The paper demonstrates several novel aspects. The application of preconditioning strategies to the Schrödinger bridge framework in speech enhancement is a unique contribution. The exploration of skip connection configurations, particularly with the clean speech target, adds a valuable ablation study to the field. Learning the noisy input contribution within the magnitude-preserving architecture further improves the conditioning of the network. Finally, the finding that shorter EMA lengths are beneficial for speech enhancement, in contrast to image generation, is a significant and potentially overlooked insight.
*   **Weaknesses:** While each component has some degree of novelty, the individual techniques (preconditioning, magnitude-preserving layers, EMA, skip connections) are known concepts, at least within related fields like image generation. The primary novelty lies in *combining* these techniques and adapting them specifically for speech enhancement within the Schrödinger bridge framework and demonstrating the impact of these combined modifications. The architectural modifications, while effective, are not a radical departure from existing U-Net based denoisers.

**Significance:**

*   **Strengths:** The work's significance lies in addressing training stability and improving the performance of diffusion-based speech enhancement. The findings regarding EMA lengths have practical implications for future research and development in this area. By demonstrating that established techniques can be adapted and optimized for the speech domain, the paper contributes to a deeper understanding of diffusion models and their application to audio processing.
*   **Weaknesses:**  The performance gains over established methods, as reflected in the benchmark results, are incremental and not statistically significant. While the paper offers valuable insights, it does not represent a breakthrough in speech enhancement performance. The real-world impact of these relatively small improvements may be limited, especially considering the computational cost associated with diffusion models. While the paper explores important design choices, its contribution is more focused on refining existing methods rather than introducing a fundamentally new paradigm.  Furthermore, the comparison benchmarks seem to be chosen and used to highlight specific improvements of the suggested design, but the improvements that are achieved are usually only marginal.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **6**.

*   **Rationale:** The paper offers several valuable contributions that move the field forward. The application of preconditioning within the Schrödinger bridge, the skip connection ablations, the learned noise injection, and the EMA analysis all contribute to a better understanding of how to train diffusion models for speech enhancement. However, the paper does not revolutionize the field. The performance gains are incremental, and the individual components, while effectively combined, are not entirely original. The analysis of the effect of EMA parameter choices on the final performance is a strong point. The work constitutes a solid engineering effort with valuable insights, but the lack of breakthrough performance and a clear paradigm shift limits its overall impact. The findings relating to EMA length could be influential in guiding future work, so there is potential for longer-term significance.

### QualBench: Benchmarking Chinese LLMs with Localized Professional Qualifications for Vertical Domain Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05225v1)
- **Authors**: Mengze Hong, Wailing Ng, Di Jiang, Chen Jason Zhang
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of Chinese large language models (LLMs) underscores the need for domain-specific evaluations to ensure reliable applications. However, existing benchmarks often lack coverage in vertical domains and offer limited insights into the Chinese working context. Leveraging qualification exams as a unified framework for human expertise evaluation, we introduce QualBench, the first multi-domain Chinese QA benchmark dedicated to localized assessment of Chinese LLMs. The dataset includes over 17,000 questions across six vertical domains, with data selections grounded in 24 Chinese qualifications to closely align with national policies and working standards. Through comprehensive evaluation, the Qwen2.5 model outperformed the more advanced GPT-4o, with Chinese LLMs consistently surpassing non-Chinese models, highlighting the importance of localized domain knowledge in meeting qualification requirements. The best performance of 75.26% reveals the current gaps in domain coverage within model capabilities. Furthermore, we present the failure of LLM collaboration with crowdsourcing mechanisms and suggest the opportunities for multi-domain RAG knowledge enhancement and vertical domain LLM training with Federated Learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "QualBench," a new multi-domain Chinese question-answering benchmark designed for evaluating the localized professional knowledge of Chinese Large Language Models (LLMs). The dataset comprises over 17,000 questions spanning six vertical domains, drawing data from 24 professional qualification exams in mainland China. The paper evaluates several popular LLMs, including both Chinese and non-Chinese models, demonstrating the importance of localized domain knowledge for achieving satisfactory performance on these qualifications.  The authors also explored LLM crowdsourcing and found it to underperform a single strong LLM. Finally, the paper suggests promising avenues for future research, including multi-domain RAG and vertical LLM training using federated learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty of the Benchmark:** QualBench addresses a critical gap in existing Chinese LLM evaluation benchmarks by focusing on localized professional knowledge and qualification exams.  Existing benchmarks often lack the required domain specificity or relevance to the Chinese working context, thus limiting their usefulness for real-world deployments in China.
    *   **Comprehensive Domain Coverage:** The benchmark covers six diverse vertical domains, providing a more comprehensive assessment of LLM capabilities than single-domain benchmarks. This makes it more relevant for practical applications that require knowledge across multiple areas.
    *   **Emphasis on Localization:** The deliberate focus on Chinese professional qualifications is a significant strength. It highlights the importance of grounding LLMs in local regulations, cultural nuances, and working standards, which are crucial for accurate and reliable performance.
    *   **Practical Insights:** The paper provides valuable insights into the performance of different LLMs on specialized knowledge tasks. The finding that Chinese LLMs consistently outperform non-Chinese models underscores the value of localized pretraining data and domain expertise. The exploration of LLM crowdsourcing is also interesting, showing the limitations of naive ensemble methods in this context.
    *   **Clear Articulation of Future Research Directions:** The paper constructively suggests promising research directions, particularly multi-domain RAG (which can address the domain coverage gap) and federated learning (which could help circumvent the data scarcity issue in vertical domains).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Imbalance:**  The significant imbalance in the number of questions across different domains (e.g., Production Safety vs. others) could skew the overall evaluation results. While the authors acknowledge this and justify it based on relevance to government policy, it still limits the generalizability of the benchmark's findings.
    *   **Limited Question Types:** The exclusive use of multiple-choice and true/false questions limits the scope of evaluation. Open-ended questions, requiring more complex reasoning and generation capabilities, could provide a more nuanced assessment of LLM understanding.
    *   **Potential Data Contamination:** The authors acknowledge the possibility of data contamination (qualification exam data being present in LLM training data), which could inflate performance scores. While difficult to avoid entirely, further investigation into this issue would be beneficial.
    *   **Crowdsourcing Simplification:** The weighted majority voting system is a relatively basic method for combining LLM results. More sophisticated ensemble techniques could potentially yield better performance. Also the prompt engineering and explanation generation components are not deeply explored, and could benefit from further elaboration.

*   **Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by providing a more relevant and challenging benchmark for evaluating Chinese LLMs. It is particularly valuable for assessing LLMs intended for deployment in professional or regulated domains within China. By highlighting the importance of localization and identifying promising research directions, the paper is likely to stimulate further research in this area.

**Overall:**

QualBench represents a useful and timely contribution to the field of LLM evaluation. While it has some limitations, the benchmark's focus on localized professional knowledge and diverse domain coverage makes it a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners working with Chinese LLMs. The insights gained from the evaluation of different models and the suggestions for future research are also noteworthy.

### ChemRxivQuest: A Curated Chemistry Question-Answer Database Extracted from ChemRxiv Preprints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05232v1)
- **Authors**: Mahmoud Amiri, Thomas Bocklitz
- **Abstract**: The rapid expansion of chemistry literature poses significant challenges for researchers seeking to efficiently access domain-specific knowledge. To support advancements in chemistry-focused natural language processing (NLP), we present ChemRxivQuest, a curated dataset of 970 high-quality question-answer (QA) pairs derived from 155 ChemRxiv preprints across 17 subfields of chemistry. Each QA pair is explicitly linked to its source text segment to ensure traceability and contextual accuracy. ChemRxivQuest was constructed using an automated pipeline that combines optical character recognition (OCR), GPT-4o-based QA generation, and a fuzzy matching technique for answer verification. The dataset emphasizes conceptual, mechanistic, applied, and experimental questions, enabling applications in retrieval-based QA systems, search engine development, and fine-tuning of domain-adapted large language models. We analyze the dataset's structure, coverage, and limitations, and outline future directions for expansion and expert validation. ChemRxivQuest provides a foundational resource for chemistry NLP research, education, and tool development.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and then critically evaluate its novelty and significance, providing a justified score.

**Summary**

The paper introduces ChemRxivQuest, a new, curated question-answer dataset specifically designed for chemistry-related natural language processing (NLP) tasks. The dataset consists of 970 high-quality question-answer pairs extracted from 155 ChemRxiv preprints covering 17 subfields of chemistry. The construction process involved optical character recognition (OCR) to extract text, GPT-4o to generate QA pairs, and a fuzzy matching algorithm to verify answer accuracy and relevance to the source text. The dataset is designed to support research in areas such as retrieval-based QA systems, search engine development, and fine-tuning domain-adapted large language models (LLMs). The paper analyzes the dataset's structure, coverage, limitations, and outlines future directions for expansion and expert validation.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field, but its novelty and significance are somewhat limited by existing resources and potential shortcomings in its construction methodology.

**Strengths:**

*   **Domain-Specific Focus:** The primary strength lies in its focus on chemistry. While other QA datasets exist, many are broad or primarily focused on biomedical literature. A dedicated chemistry dataset fills a crucial gap and addresses the unique challenges posed by chemical language and knowledge.

*   **Structured QA Format:** The carefully structured QA format, with explicit links to the original source text, allows for enhanced traceability, validation, and utilization in various NLP applications. The inclusion of source snippets is valuable for RAG approaches.

*   **Automated Pipeline:** The automated pipeline used for dataset construction, while presenting some limitations, allows for scalability and the potential for future expansion. The use of state-of-the-art LLMs for QA generation ensures a certain level of quality and relevance.

*   **Comprehensive Topical Coverage:** The inclusion of 17 chemistry subfields contributes to a diverse dataset that reflects the breadth of modern chemical research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Preprints:** The exclusive use of ChemRxiv preprints raises concerns about data quality and reliability. Preprints have not undergone peer review, and the information they contain may be inaccurate, incomplete, or subject to change.

*   **Potential for Hallucinations:** The generation of QA pairs using LLMs carries the risk of hallucinations, where the model generates answers that are not supported by the source text or are factually incorrect. While the paper addresses this concern with fuzzy matching, it might not capture subtle semantic errors. This is a *critical* point, as factual accuracy is paramount in scientific applications.

*   **Fuzzy Matching Limitations:** The reliance on fuzzy matching for answer verification is a limitation. Fuzzy matching prioritizes textual similarity over semantic correctness, which could lead to the acceptance of syntactically correct but contextually invalid answers.

*   **Limited Human Validation:** The manual review of only 10% of the retained QA pairs may not be sufficient to ensure high accuracy across the entire dataset. A more thorough human validation process would significantly improve the dataset's reliability.

*   **Lack of Comparative Analysis:** The paper lacks a thorough comparative analysis with existing relevant datasets. While it mentions some existing datasets, it doesn't provide a detailed comparison of their strengths and weaknesses relative to ChemRxivQuest.
*   **Limited Quantitative evaluation of QA quality:** while an estimated accuracy of 95% is mentioned post-filtering, further evaluation with established QA datasets would greatly strengthen the work.

**Novelty:** The novelty is primarily in the application of a modern LLM-driven QA generation pipeline to the specific domain of chemistry preprints and in creating a single, relatively large, curated QA dataset of this type for chemistry. However, the individual components of the pipeline (OCR, LLM-based QA, fuzzy matching) are not novel in themselves.

**Significance:** The significance lies in providing a resource to facilitate research and development in chemistry-specific NLP. It can contribute to improving QA systems, fine-tuning LLMs, and evaluating knowledge extraction techniques. However, the limitations mentioned above could limit its widespread adoption and impact. Also, the dataset is of moderate size, while much larger resources may be needed to effectively train and validate modern NLP systems.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, a score of **6** is justified. The dataset fills a gap in chemistry-specific NLP resources, but the reliance on preprints and the limitations of the validation approach diminish its overall quality and trustworthiness. While useful, it's not a groundbreaking advancement in the field and requires further refinement, particularly human validation, to significantly impact the quality and reliability of chemistry-focused NLP. The potential for hallucinated answers and the limited scope of manual review, are crucial factors justifying a lower score. While useful, the work suffers from limitations in data selection and accuracy, and is not yet a major leap forward.

### Latte: Transfering LLMs` Latent-level Knowledge for Few-shot Tabular Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05237v1)
- **Authors**: Ruxue Shi, Hengrui Gu, Hangting Ye, Yiwei Dai, Xu Shen, Xin Wang
- **Abstract**: Few-shot tabular learning, in which machine learning models are trained with a limited amount of labeled data, provides a cost-effective approach to addressing real-world challenges. The advent of Large Language Models (LLMs) has sparked interest in leveraging their pre-trained knowledge for few-shot tabular learning. Despite promising results, existing approaches either rely on test-time knowledge extraction, which introduces undesirable latency, or text-level knowledge, which leads to unreliable feature engineering. To overcome these limitations, we propose Latte, a training-time knowledge extraction framework that transfers the latent prior knowledge within LLMs to optimize a more generalized downstream model. Latte enables general knowledge-guided downstream tabular learning, facilitating the weighted fusion of information across different feature values while reducing the risk of overfitting to limited labeled data. Furthermore, Latte is compatible with existing unsupervised pre-training paradigms and effectively utilizes available unlabeled samples to overcome the performance limitations imposed by an extremely small labeled dataset. Extensive experiments on various few-shot tabular learning benchmarks demonstrate the superior performance of Latte, establishing it as a state-of-the-art approach in this domain
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Latte: Transfering LLMs' Latent-level Knowledge for Few-shot Tabular Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Latte, a novel framework for few-shot tabular learning that leverages the latent-level knowledge of Large Language Models (LLMs).  Unlike existing methods that rely on test-time knowledge extraction (introducing latency) or text-level knowledge (leading to unreliable feature engineering), Latte extracts knowledge during training. It positions the LLM as a "teacher" to guide the training of a downstream tabular learning model.  Latte comprises a semantic-aware tabular encoder, a knowledge adapter to fuse latent knowledge, and an optional unsupervised pre-training stage to utilize unlabeled data effectively. The framework is evaluated on several few-shot tabular learning benchmarks, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the approach of using *latent-level* knowledge extraction from LLMs during training, as opposed to *text-level* rule generation or inference-time querying.  The design of the semantic-aware encoder and the knowledge adapter are also key contributions, facilitating the effective transfer of knowledge from LLMs to the tabular data model. The inclusion of unsupervised pre-training with pseudo-labeling to improve performance with limited labeled data is also a valuable addition. It uses pseudo labels based on unsupervised clustering, which can be useful for making use of unlabeled data. The specific methodology of combining the semantic-aware encoder, latent knowledge distillation, and pseudo-labeling provides a unique approach to few-shot tabular learning with LLMs.

*   **Significance:** Few-shot tabular learning is a practical problem with real-world applications where labeled data is scarce and expensive. The potential to harness LLMs to address this challenge is significant, and Latte offers a promising approach. The improvement in performance demonstrated on various datasets shows the potential of the proposed method in real-world applications.
The study offers a step forward in mitigating the limitations of test-time knowledge extraction. By addressing the lack of usage of available unlabeled data, the paper contributes towards improving the model robustness and parameter initialization.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing LLM-based few-shot tabular learning methods (latency, unreliable knowledge extraction).
    *   The proposed Latte framework is well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, demonstrating superior performance on several benchmark datasets.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component of the framework.
    *   Addresses the challenge of few-shot tabular learning effectively.
    *   Well-written, with a clear structure and detailed explanations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on metadata could be a limitation in scenarios where metadata is incomplete or inaccurate. The method relies on effective prompt engineering to extract meaningful task-relevant knowledge. The prompt engineering to produce metadata may vary, depending on which method you use. The prompt may not perfectly capture nuances in the dataset.

    *   While the paper demonstrates improvements, the absolute performance gains might be dataset-dependent, and scalability to very high-dimensional tabular data isn't thoroughly explored.
    *   The choice of LLaMA2 as the LLM backbone restricts the analysis.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a well-designed and well-evaluated framework that makes a significant contribution to few-shot tabular learning. Latte addresses the limitations of previous LLM-based approaches and offers a practical solution for learning from limited data. While there are some limitations regarding metadata reliance and potential scalability issues, the novelty and significance of the approach justify a high score.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** Latte introduces a valuable improvement in applying LLMs to few-shot tabular learning, providing a unique training-time approach that leverages latent knowledge, thus mitigating previous test-time latency issues. Its demonstrated improvements across several datasets suggest its applicability. However, the reliance on quality metadata and limitations from the LLaMA2 LLM used limit the maximum possible score. More analysis is needed to see if the task relevant knowledge extracted through prompt engineering is the best.


- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### T-T: Table Transformer for Tagging-based Aspect Sentiment Triplet Extraction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05271v1)
- **Authors**: Kun Peng, Chaodong Tong, Cong Cao, Hao Peng, Qian Li, Guanlin Wu, Lei Jiang, Yanbing Liu, Philip S. Yu
- **Abstract**: Aspect sentiment triplet extraction (ASTE) aims to extract triplets composed of aspect terms, opinion terms, and sentiment polarities from given sentences. The table tagging method is a popular approach to addressing this task, which encodes a sentence into a 2-dimensional table, allowing for the tagging of relations between any two words. Previous efforts have focused on designing various downstream relation learning modules to better capture interactions between tokens in the table, revealing that a stronger capability to capture relations can lead to greater improvements in the model. Motivated by this, we attempt to directly utilize transformer layers as downstream relation learning modules. Due to the powerful semantic modeling capability of transformers, it is foreseeable that this will lead to excellent improvement. However, owing to the quadratic relation between the length of the table and the length of the input sentence sequence, using transformers directly faces two challenges: overly long table sequences and unfair local attention interaction. To address these challenges, we propose a novel Table-Transformer (T-T) for the tagging-based ASTE method. Specifically, we introduce a stripe attention mechanism with a loop-shift strategy to tackle these challenges. The former modifies the global attention mechanism to only attend to a 2-dimensional local attention window, while the latter facilitates interaction between different attention windows. Extensive and comprehensive experiments demonstrate that the T-T, as a downstream relation learning module, achieves state-of-the-art performance with lower computational costs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel "Table-Transformer" (T-T) architecture for the Aspect Sentiment Triplet Extraction (ASTE) task, which is an important aspect of fine-grained sentiment analysis.  ASTE aims to extract triplets of (aspect term, opinion term, sentiment polarity) from sentences. T-T is designed to improve upon existing table tagging methods, a popular approach to ASTE. The key contributions are:

1.  **Stripe Attention Mechanism:** Restricts the global self-attention mechanism of transformers to a local, 2D attention window. This reduces the computational complexity associated with long table sequences created from the input sentences.
2.  **Loop-Shift Strategy:**  Facilitates interaction between different attention windows in the stripe attention mechanism.  This is designed to address issues of unfair local attention interaction, where tokens at the edges of attention windows might miss important relationships with tokens in adjacent windows.
The paper presents experimental results on benchmark datasets, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance compared to other approaches, along with computational cost analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the integration of stripe attention and loop-shift strategy within a transformer architecture specifically tailored for the table tagging approach in ASTE.  While stripe attention (or similar local attention mechanisms) and shifting strategies have been explored in other contexts (e.g., image processing, long sequence modeling), their combination and application to the *table* representation for ASTE is the primary novel contribution.The idea of utilising the transformer and improving performance for the ASTE tasks is not extremely novel given that this field has been researched for some time. The main point is to improve the tagging methods which has limitations.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a practical problem: the high computational cost of using transformers with table tagging methods for ASTE. By reducing the complexity with stripe attention and loop-shift strategies, the authors make it feasible to apply transformer-based approaches to longer sequences. This leads to improved performance without significantly increased cost.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmark datasets, which is a significant indicator of the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The detailed ablation studies further support the value of both the stripe attention and loop-shift mechanisms.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing methods in terms of computational cost and unfair attention interaction.
    *   **Well-Explained Methodology:** The description of the stripe attention and loop-shift strategies is clear and understandable.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes ablation studies and hyperparameter analysis to provide a comprehensive understanding of the model's behavior.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of stripe attention and loop-shift is novel within the specific context of table tagging for ASTE, the individual components have precedents in other areas.
    *   **Complexity:** The model has a substantial number of parameters (184.3M), raising concerns about its resource requirements. While the authors address the computational *complexity* of the attention mechanism, the sheer size of the model could still be a barrier to wider adoption.
    *   **Limited Ablation:** The ablation study removes the "T-T Relation Encoder", what the study really needs is to remove the transformer itself to see how performance compares to some earlier work as an ablation study.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in ASTE by providing a more efficient way to leverage the power of transformers. The stripe attention and loop-shift strategies could be adapted for other sequence labeling tasks that involve long sequences or table-like representations.  The detailed ablation studies and analysis of different components also provides useful insights for researchers working on similar problems.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a technically sound and well-evaluated approach to addressing a practical problem in ASTE. The combination of stripe attention and loop-shift for table tagging is novel within this specific context. The empirical results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods. The paper is well-written and provides a comprehensive analysis of the model's behavior.

However, the score is not higher because the individual components (stripe attention and loop shifting) are not entirely new concepts, and the model's parameter count is relatively high, even with the computational efficiency improvements. Furthermore, improvements over other approaches, while there, are incremental and not dramatic. There are also a few missing ablation studies to show a better comparison to earlier work.

Therefore, a score of 7 reflects a valuable contribution with demonstrated effectiveness and potential for further impact, but acknowledges the limitations in terms of fundamental novelty and model complexity.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Software Development Life Cycle Perspective: A Survey of Benchmarks for Code Large Language Models and Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05283v2)
- **Authors**: Kaixin Wang, Tianlin Li, Xiaoyu Zhang, Chong Wang, Weisong Sun, Yang Liu, Bin Shi
- **Abstract**: Code large language models (CodeLLMs) and agents have shown great promise in tackling complex software engineering tasks.Compared to traditional software engineering methods, CodeLLMs and agents offer stronger abilities, and can flexibly process inputs and outputs in both natural and code. Benchmarking plays a crucial role in evaluating the capabilities of CodeLLMs and agents, guiding their development and deployment. However, despite their growing significance, there remains a lack of comprehensive reviews of benchmarks for CodeLLMs and agents. To bridge this gap, this paper provides a comprehensive review of existing benchmarks for CodeLLMs and agents, studying and analyzing 181 benchmarks from 461 relevant papers, covering the different phases of the software development life cycle (SDLC). Our findings reveal a notable imbalance in the coverage of current benchmarks, with approximately 60% focused on the software development phase in SDLC, while requirements engineering and software design phases receive minimal attention at only 5% and 3%, respectively. Additionally, Python emerges as the dominant programming language across the reviewed benchmarks. Finally, this paper highlights the challenges of current research and proposes future directions, aiming to narrow the gap between the theoretical capabilities of CodeLLMs and agents and their application in real-world scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Software Development Life Cycle Perspective: A Survey of Benchmarks for Code Large Language Models and Agents" provides a comprehensive survey of existing benchmarks used to evaluate code large language models (CodeLLMs) and agents. The authors analyzed 181 benchmarks from 461 papers, categorizing them according to the phases of the Software Development Life Cycle (SDLC). Their analysis reveals a significant imbalance in benchmark coverage, with a strong focus on the software development phase and comparatively less attention given to requirements engineering and software design. The study also identifies Python as the dominant programming language in the reviewed benchmarks. Finally, the paper highlights challenges in current research and proposes future directions to bridge the gap between theoretical CodeLLM capabilities and real-world applications.  The authors suggest a range of improvements related to non-functional capabilities, multi-agent collaboration, human-in-the-loop frameworks, and end-to-end testing across the entire SDLC.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel perspective by organizing and analyzing existing benchmarks through the lens of the SDLC. While surveys of code generation and related tasks exist, focusing specifically on *benchmarks* across *all* phases of the SDLC with an eye towards *CodeLLM agents* gives this paper added value. The identified imbalance in SDLC coverage is a useful contribution.
*   **Significance:** This survey fills a critical gap in understanding the current state of benchmark development for CodeLLMs and agents. By highlighting the limitations of existing benchmarks, the paper provides valuable guidance for researchers and practitioners aiming to develop more comprehensive and representative evaluation frameworks. The future directions proposed are also significant for guiding future research efforts towards addressing the identified gaps. The discussion of MLLMs and multi-agent systems is also timely and relevant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of benchmarks and provides a detailed categorization based on the SDLC.
    *   **Clear Structure:** The paper is well-organized and clearly articulates the research questions, methodology, findings, and future directions.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The identified gaps and proposed future directions provide valuable insights for researchers and practitioners in the field.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Depth of Analysis:** While the breadth is impressive, some sections could benefit from a deeper analysis of individual benchmarks, perhaps including concrete examples of limitations or biases. The scoring is based only on *frequency* and not weighted for importance/difficulty.
    *   **Subjectivity in Categorization:** Categorizing benchmarks within the SDLC phases may involve some level of subjectivity, and the authors could acknowledge this potential limitation and discuss the rationale behind their classifications. It seems the definition of "benchmarks that evaluate the capabilities of agents" is subjective as well.
    *   **Lack of Empirical Validation:** The paper mainly presents a qualitative analysis. While the proposed directions are logical, some could potentially benefit from further empirical support to confirm their effectiveness.
    *   **Static Landscape:** The field of LLMs and benchmarks is rapidly evolving. It may be difficult for a survey to remain entirely current for an extended period.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of new benchmarks and evaluation frameworks for CodeLLMs and agents. By highlighting the gaps in current research, it encourages researchers to focus on under-explored areas and develop more comprehensive evaluation methodologies. The emphasis on real-world scenarios and diverse tasks will likely contribute to the development of more practical and relevant benchmarks.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by systematically surveying and categorizing the landscape of benchmarks for CodeLLMs and agents. The identification of gaps in SDLC coverage, along with the future directions, is valuable for the research community. However, the lack of in-depth analysis in some areas and the static nature of the field limit the overall impact. While comprehensive, it lacks some critical depth in examining the trade-offs and biases present in the scoring. It could potentially become an influential document for researchers but stops short of being groundbreaking.

### HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL: Optimizing LLM Inference Request Scheduling for Agentic Text-to-SQL Workflow
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05286v1)
- **Authors**: You Peng, Youhe Jiang, Chen Wang, Binhang Yuan
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in leveraging the agentic paradigm of large language models (LLMs) utilization have significantly enhanced Text-to-SQL capabilities, enabling users without specialized database expertise to query data intuitively. However, deploying these agentic LLM-based Text-to-SQL systems in production poses substantial challenges due to their inherently multi-stage workflows, stringent latency constraints, and potentially heterogeneous GPU infrastructure in enterprise environments. Current LLM serving frameworks lack effective mechanisms for handling interdependent inference tasks, dynamic latency variability, and resource heterogeneity, leading to suboptimal performance and frequent service-level objective (SLO) violations. In this paper, we introduce HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL, a novel framework designed explicitly to schedule and execute agentic multi-stage LLM-based Text-to-SQL workflows on heterogeneous GPU clusters that handle multi-tenant end-to-end queries. HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL introduce a hierarchical scheduling approach combining global workload-balanced task dispatching and local adaptive urgency-guided prioritization, guided by a systematic analysis of agentic Text-to-SQL workflows. Additionally, we propose a lightweight simulation-based method for tuning critical scheduling hyperparameters, further enhancing robustness and adaptability. Our extensive evaluation on realistic Text-to-SQL benchmarks demonstrates that HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL significantly outperforms state-of-the-art LLM serving frameworks. Specifically, HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL reduces latency deadlines by up to 1.67$\times$ (average: 1.41$\times$) and improves system throughput by up to 1.75$\times$ (average: 1.65$\times$) compared to vLLM under diverse, realistic workload conditions. Our code is available at https://github.com/Relaxed-System-Lab/Hexgen-Flow.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL: Optimizing LLM Inference Request Scheduling for Agentic Text-to-SQL Workflows."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of deploying agentic LLM-based Text-to-SQL systems in production, focusing on the need for efficient scheduling of multi-stage inference requests, particularly in heterogeneous GPU environments. The authors introduce HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL, a novel framework designed to schedule and execute these workflows, considering inter-stage dependencies, latency variability, and resource heterogeneity. HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL employs a hierarchical scheduling approach with global workload-balanced task dispatching and local adaptive urgency-guided prioritization. It also utilizes a lightweight simulation-based method for tuning scheduling hyperparameters. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL outperforms state-of-the-art LLM serving frameworks (vLLM) in terms of latency deadline reduction and system throughput.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strength:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its explicit focus on the scheduling challenges specific to *agentic* LLM-based Text-to-SQL workflows. Previous LLM serving frameworks often treat inference tasks as independent, neglecting the multi-stage nature and dependencies inherent in these workflows. The hierarchical scheduling approach is a reasonable, though not revolutionary, solution to these challenges.The introduction of the lightweight simulation for hyperparameter tuning also adds incremental value.

    *   **Weakness:** While the integration of workload-balanced task dispatching with the urgency-guided prioritization does address important aspects of this scheduling problem, these aspects have already been well-studied, and this approach only introduces them to LLMs. It can also be argued that some components, such as the workload-balanced dispatcher, leverage techniques already present in other LLM serving research (some have been referenced by the author). Therefore, the novelty is somewhat incremental; it's a targeted application of existing principles to a specific problem domain.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strength:** The paper addresses a practical and increasingly important problem. Agentic LLM-based systems are gaining traction, and their efficient deployment is critical for real-world applications. The demonstrated improvements in latency and throughput compared to existing frameworks suggest that HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL can have a tangible impact on the performance and usability of these systems. The design principles outlined in the paper provide valuable guidance for future research in this area. The provision of open source code for this project is useful for future work in this area.

    *   **Weakness:** The experimental evaluation, while thorough, is limited to a specific Text-to-SQL framework (CHESS) and a particular set of LLMs and hardware configurations. The generalizability of the results to other agentic LLM systems and more diverse hardware environments needs further investigation. While it claims state-of-the-art performance improvement, there are some claims in the paper that are not clearly substantiated, such as bettering all other similar approaches; in general it is an ambitious claim that requires some rigorous analysis and benchmark.

*   **Rigor:**

    *   **Strength:** The paper provides a clear and well-defined problem statement, a detailed description of the HEXGEN-TEXT2SQL framework, and a comprehensive experimental evaluation with multiple baselines and performance metrics.
    *   **Weakness:** The paper uses CHESS, which is state-of-the-art by the time the authors of this submission writes the paper, but given how fast LLM models progress, there are many newer models, and newer LLM models may yield new system bottlenecks that the design may need to consider.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6.5**. While the paper addresses a significant problem and presents a reasonable solution with demonstrated improvements, the novelty is somewhat incremental, relying on existing scheduling principles and applying them to the specific context of agentic LLM-based Text-to-SQL workflows. The experimental evaluation is thorough but could benefit from broader validation across different systems and hardware configurations. Given that many aspects of the scheduling solution are extensions of pre-existing approaches and the generalizability could be questioned, it does not qualify as a truly exceptional contribution that would merit a higher score.

### Benchmarking Ophthalmology Foundation Models for Clinically Significant Age Macular Degeneration Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05291v1)
- **Authors**: Benjamin A. Cohen, Jonathan Fhima, Meishar Meisel, Baskin Meital, Luis Filipe Nakayama, Eran Berkowitz, Joachim A. Behar
- **Abstract**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) has enabled Vision Transformers (ViTs) to learn robust representations from large-scale natural image datasets, enhancing their generalization across domains. In retinal imaging, foundation models pretrained on either natural or ophthalmic data have shown promise, but the benefits of in-domain pretraining remain uncertain. To investigate this, we benchmark six SSL-pretrained ViTs on seven digital fundus image (DFI) datasets totaling 70,000 expert-annotated images for the task of moderate-to-late age-related macular degeneration (AMD) identification. Our results show that iBOT pretrained on natural images achieves the highest out-of-distribution generalization, with AUROCs of 0.80-0.97, outperforming domain-specific models, which achieved AUROCs of 0.78-0.96 and a baseline ViT-L with no pretraining, which achieved AUROCs of 0.68-0.91. These findings highlight the value of foundation models in improving AMD identification and challenge the assumption that in-domain pretraining is necessary. Furthermore, we release BRAMD, an open-access dataset (n=587) of DFIs with AMD labels from Brazil.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll summarize and critically evaluate the paper.

**Summary:**

This paper benchmarks several self-supervised learning (SSL) pretrained Vision Transformers (ViTs) for the task of identifying intermediate-to-late age-related macular degeneration (AMD) in digital fundus images (DFIs). The authors evaluated six foundation models pretrained on both natural images and ophthalmic images across seven DFI datasets. They found that iBOT, a ViT pretrained on natural images, achieved the highest out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization performance.  The authors also introduced a new open-access DFI dataset called BRAMD. They further refined iBOT with a multi-source domain training approach, creating a model called AMDNet, which outperformed a state-of-the-art open-source AMD detection model. The paper includes error analysis, exploring performance variations across AMD subgroups, demographic factors, and comorbidities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty is mixed. Benchmarking different foundation models is valuable, but it's not a completely novel undertaking. The specific *combination* of models tested and the focus on OOD generalization increases its novelty.  The finding that a model pretrained on natural images outperforms those pretrained on ophthalmic data challenges existing assumptions in the field and adds a valuable, counterintuitive insight. The creation and public release of the BRAMD dataset is a clear positive contribution.  The multi-source domain training approach is described as based on previous work, reducing its individual novelty, though its application here is relevant.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper lies in several key areas:

*   **Challenging the Status Quo:** The central finding that natural image pretraining outperforms in-domain pretraining for this task is significant because it directly contradicts the current trend of developing specialized retinal foundation models. This could lead to a rethinking of resource allocation and research directions.

*   **OOD Generalization:** The emphasis on OOD generalization is crucial for real-world clinical applications. Medical imaging AI models often struggle when deployed on data from different hospitals or imaging devices. This paper tackles that problem head-on.

*   **Open-Access Dataset:** The release of the BRAMD dataset is a valuable resource for the research community, allowing other researchers to validate the authors' findings and develop new models.

*   **AMDNet Performance:** The AMDNet model demonstrating an improvement over an existing SOTA model provides a practical contribution.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Benchmarking:** The paper compares a substantial number of foundation models on a large and diverse set of DFI datasets.
*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The authors use appropriate evaluation metrics (AUROC), bootstrapping for statistical significance, and careful attention to data preprocessing.
*   **Well-Written and Clear:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.
*   **Detailed Error Analysis:** The paper includes a thorough error analysis, examining performance variations across subgroups, demographics, and comorbidities. This provides valuable insights into the model's strengths and weaknesses.
*   **Reproducibility**: The public release of the BRAMD dataset and AMDNet improves the reproducibility of the findings.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvement:** While AMDNet outperforms existing models, the improvement, while non-incremental in DeepSeeNet's case, isn't a *massive* leap in performance.
*   **Limited Explainability:** As the authors acknowledge, the explainability of the model is limited. While Grad-CAM is used, it doesn't provide detailed insights into the specific features the model is using to make its decisions.  A more in-depth feature attribution analysis would be beneficial.
*   **Dataset focus:** Although the number of images is substantial, there is a focus on the US population, which is not reflective of other populations around the world.
*   **Limited Scope of Analysis**: The focus is on DFIs with AMD and controls. Other retinal diseases were only examined for the error analysis but could have been included to broaden the significance.

**Justification for Score:**

While the work is not a revolutionary breakthrough, it makes several important contributions to the field. The challenging of pre-existing assumption of natural imaging is highly significant. The finding that models trained on natural images outperform those trained on ophthalmic images is something that is counterintuitive. However, this comes with the consideration that AMDNet only slightly outperformed the SOTA model.

### Toward Reasonable Parrots: Why Large Language Models Should Argue with Us by Design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05298v1)
- **Authors**: Elena Musi, Nadin Kokciyan, Khalid Al-Khatib, Davide Ceolin, Emmanuelle Dietz, Klara Gutekunst, Annette Hautli-Janisz, Cristian Manuel Santibañez Yañez, Jodi Schneider, Jonas Scholz, Cor Steging, Jacky Visser, Henning Wachsmuth
- **Abstract**: In this position paper, we advocate for the development of conversational technology that is inherently designed to support and facilitate argumentative processes. We argue that, at present, large language models (LLMs) are inadequate for this purpose, and we propose an ideal technology design aimed at enhancing argumentative skills. This involves re-framing LLMs as tools to exercise our critical thinking rather than replacing them. We introduce the concept of 'reasonable parrots' that embody the fundamental principles of relevance, responsibility, and freedom, and that interact through argumentative dialogical moves. These principles and moves arise out of millennia of work in argumentation theory and should serve as the starting point for LLM-based technology that incorporates basic principles of argumentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a new direction for large language model (LLM) development, moving away from "stochastic parrots" (systems that merely mimic patterns in data) towards "reasonable parrots." These reasonable parrots would be designed to facilitate and enhance argumentative processes, fostering critical thinking in users. The authors argue that current LLMs fall short of this goal, often exhibiting biases and fallacies. They outline principles of relevance, responsibility, and freedom that reasonable parrots should embody, and sketch a multi-parrot design with distinct argumentative roles to promote deliberation and diverse perspectives.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its explicit focus on integrating argumentation theory principles *into the design* of LLMs. While previous research explores LLM reasoning abilities and their use in specific domains (like decision making), this paper advocates for a fundamental re-framing of the technology's purpose: as a tool for critical thinking rather than a replacement for it.  The "reasonable parrot" metaphor, although evocative, is built upon established critiques of LLMs (stochastic parrots), but the explicit link to argumentation theory and the proposed design elements (multi-parrot personas) provide a fresh perspective. However, the multi-parrot idea is quite high-level, and there is no actual implementation or detailed architecture suggested.  The connection to real-world argumentative tasks, beyond the smartphone example, isn't strongly developed.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial concern: the potential for LLMs to reinforce biases and stifle critical thought.  Given the increasing use of LLMs in decision-making, education, and information access, the need for technologies that promote deliberation and critical evaluation is significant. The paper raises awareness of the limitations of current LLMs in this respect and offers a starting point for designing more responsible and beneficial AI systems. However, the impact is somewhat limited by the lack of concrete implementation or evaluation. The discussion on why this design is beneficial, in particular on the actual argumentative capability of an LLM, is vague.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear articulation of the problem with current LLMs as "stochastic parrots."
    *   Connection to established principles of argumentation theory (relevance, responsibility, freedom).
    *   Introduction of a concrete, if high-level, design concept (multi-parrot personas).
    *   Addresses a timely and important societal concern about the impact of LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The "multi-parrot" design is conceptual and lacks detailed implementation specifications.
    *   Limited evaluation or evidence to support the effectiveness of the proposed design. The example dialogue is illustrative but doesn't demonstrate the system's robustness or impact on user thinking.
    *   The paper relies heavily on anecdotal examples and conceptual arguments, lacking empirical data or rigorous analysis of existing systems.
    *   The paper doesn't adequately address the considerable engineering challenges of implementing such a system with current LLM technologies and techniques.
    *   It assumes that LLMs, as "parrots," can embody argumentative roles, and it's not obvious they do more than imitate them.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the direction of future LLM research by encouraging developers to prioritize critical thinking and argumentation skills in system design. The "reasonable parrot" metaphor could become a shorthand for responsible LLM development, similar to the "stochastic parrot" critique. The paper could stimulate further research on integrating argumentation theory into AI systems and on evaluating the impact of such systems on user reasoning abilities.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and timely perspective on LLM development. It identifies a critical gap in current systems and proposes a potentially fruitful approach to address it. However, its impact is limited by the lack of concrete implementation or evaluation, as well as the high-level nature of the design proposal. The novelty of simply putting argumentation theory into LLMs is also debatable.

### ICon: In-Context Contribution for Automatic Data Selection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05327v1)
- **Authors**: Yixin Yang, Qingxiu Dong, Linli Yao, Fangwei Zhu, Zhifang Sui
- **Abstract**: Data selection for instruction tuning is essential for improving the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) and reducing training cost. However, existing automated selection methods either depend on computationally expensive gradient-based measures or manually designed heuristics, which may fail to fully exploit the intrinsic attributes of data. In this paper, we propose In-context Learning for Contribution Measurement (ICon), a novel gradient-free method that takes advantage of the implicit fine-tuning nature of in-context learning (ICL) to measure sample contribution without gradient computation or manual indicators engineering. ICon offers a computationally efficient alternative to gradient-based methods and reduces human inductive bias inherent in heuristic-based approaches. ICon comprises three components and identifies high-contribution data by assessing performance shifts under implicit learning through ICL. Extensive experiments on three LLMs across 12 benchmarks and 5 pairwise evaluation sets demonstrate the effectiveness of ICon. Remarkably, on LLaMA3.1-8B, models trained on 15% of ICon-selected data outperform full datasets by 5.42% points and exceed the best performance of widely used selection methods by 2.06% points. We further analyze high-contribution samples selected by ICon, which show both diverse tasks and appropriate difficulty levels, rather than just the hardest ones.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ICON: In-Context Contribution for Automatic Data Selection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ICON (In-Context Contribution for Measurement), a novel gradient-free method for automated data selection for instruction tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs). ICON leverages the in-context learning (ICL) ability of LLMs to estimate the contribution of individual training samples. It works by constructing an assessment set, computing an ICON score for each sample based on performance shifts via ICL, and then training a lightweight model (using LoRA) to select high-contribution samples for fine-tuning.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of ICON on three LLMs (LLaMA3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-3B, and LLaMA2-7B) across 12 benchmarks and 5 pairwise evaluation sets, showing that models trained on a smaller fraction of ICON-selected data can outperform those trained on the full dataset and exceed existing selection methods. They analyze characteristics of high-contribution samples, finding a mix of tasks and difficulty levels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using ICL to estimate sample contribution for data selection is novel.  Traditional methods rely on gradients (computationally expensive) or manually designed heuristics (introducing bias). ICON offers a compelling alternative that is both gradient-free and reduces reliance on handcrafted features. The three-component approach (assessment set, ICON score, and selection paradigm) is also well-structured and contributes to the overall novelty.

*   **Significance:** Data selection is a critical area in LLM research, particularly for improving performance and reducing training costs. ICON addresses a significant challenge by providing a computationally efficient and less biased way to identify valuable training data. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate that ICON can significantly improve the performance of LLMs while using a fraction of the training data. This has direct implications for reducing resource consumption and making LLM training more accessible. The analysis of the selected data provides valuable insights into what makes a "good" training sample.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results across multiple models, benchmarks, and datasets. The consistent outperformance of ICON-selected data is convincing.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:**  The gradient-free nature of ICON makes it a practical alternative to gradient-based data selection methods, especially for large datasets and models.
    *   **Reduced Bias:** By leveraging ICL, ICON reduces human inductive bias compared to heuristic-based data selection techniques.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The analysis of high-contribution samples provides valuable insights beyond simply showing performance gains. This enhances the impact and understanding of the method.
    *   **Reproducibility**: The authors have released the project page with code.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on ICL:**  The method's performance is inherently tied to the effectiveness of ICL for the target model.  ICL's behavior can be complex and sensitive to prompt design, potentially introducing some variability. The prompt used for ICL is fixed; investigation on the optimal prompt will provide more insights.
    *   **Assessment Set Construction:** The performance of ICON relies on a well-constructed assessment set. Although the paper constructs the assessment set from various sources and ensures no overlap with training datasets, the authors should mention the construction of assessment set is still a laborious process. How the quality of the dataset affects ICON is unknown.
    *   **Alpaca-centric**:  The experimental setup and analysis largely focus on the Alpaca dataset and its variants. While this provides a controlled environment, evaluating on more diverse and challenging datasets (e.g., real-world tasks) would further strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   **Limited comparison:** The baselines selected for comparison are rather limited. More recent data selection methods, especially those using lightweight heuristics, are missing. Also, the comparison is limited to benchmarks. Qualitative analysis of generated responses is lacking.

*   **Potential Influence:**  ICON has the potential to significantly impact the field of LLM instruction tuning.  Its computational efficiency, reduced bias, and strong performance gains make it a promising approach for selecting training data. Other researchers can leverage ICON's insights to develop new and improved data selection techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

ICON introduces a truly novel method that addresses an important problem in LLM instruction tuning. The paper provides a thorough experimental evaluation and a valuable analysis of the results. The approach is computationally efficient and tackles the issue of human bias in data selection. The limitations, while present, do not significantly detract from the paper's overall contribution. The method appears directly applicable and impactful. Thus,

### Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models for Coastal Inundation Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05381v1)
- **Authors**: Kazi Ashik Islam, Zakaria Mehrab, Mahantesh Halappanavar, Henning Mortveit, Sridhar Katragadda, Jon Derek Loftis, Madhav Marathe
- **Abstract**: Coastal flooding poses significant risks to communities, necessitating fast and accurate forecasting methods to mitigate potential damage. To approach this problem, we present DIFF-FLOOD, a probabilistic spatiotemporal forecasting method designed based on denoising diffusion models. DIFF-FLOOD predicts inundation level at a location by taking both spatial and temporal context into account. It utilizes inundation levels at neighboring locations and digital elevation data as spatial context. Inundation history from a context time window, together with additional co-variates are used as temporal context. Convolutional neural networks and cross-attention mechanism are then employed to capture the spatiotemporal dynamics in the data. We trained and tested DIFF-FLOOD on coastal inundation data from the Eastern Shore of Virginia, a region highly impacted by coastal flooding. Our results show that, DIFF-FLOOD outperforms existing forecasting methods in terms of prediction performance (6% to 64% improvement in terms of two performance metrics) and scalability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DIFF-FLOOD, a novel probabilistic spatiotemporal forecasting method for coastal inundation based on denoising diffusion models.  DIFF-FLOOD predicts inundation levels by leveraging both spatial context (inundation levels at neighboring locations and digital elevation data) and temporal context (historical inundation data and other co-variates). The model employs convolutional neural networks and cross-attention mechanisms to capture spatiotemporal dynamics.  The authors trained and tested DIFF-FLOOD on data from the Eastern Shore of Virginia and demonstrated that it outperforms existing forecasting methods in terms of prediction performance and scalability. The paper also discusses the model's ability to answer scenario-based queries relevant to policymakers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of denoising diffusion models to coastal inundation forecasting is, in itself, novel.  While diffusion models have been used for time-series forecasting and spatiotemporal graph forecasting, DIFF-FLOOD distinguishes itself by focusing on the specific challenges of high-resolution coastal inundation prediction and incorporating both spatial and temporal contexts in a manner that addresses scalability issues present in other approaches. The cross-attention mechanism and the UNet architecture tailored for this specific task appear to be well-designed for the problem.

*   **Significance:** Accurate and scalable coastal inundation forecasting is a significant problem with direct policy implications. The paper clearly articulates this motivation and emphasizes the real-world relevance of the research. The performance improvements reported (6% to 64% depending on the metric and configuration) compared to existing baselines are substantial and suggest that DIFF-FLOOD has the potential to be a useful tool for coastal resource management and risk prevention. The model's ability to answer scenario-based queries directly addresses the needs of policymakers, increasing its practical significance. The demonstration of scalability addresses a critical limitation of previous models, making DIFF-FLOOD potentially deployable in large-scale, real-time forecasting scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem formulation:** The paper clearly defines the problem of coastal inundation forecasting and its importance.
    *   **Well-motivated approach:** The use of denoising diffusion models is justified, highlighting their suitability for incorporating various conditioning contexts and modeling physical properties.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The authors conducted a thorough evaluation of DIFF-FLOOD against several baseline methods, including state-of-the-art approaches. The ablation study further strengthens the evaluation by demonstrating the importance of individual components.
    *   **Scalability demonstration:** The experiments showing the scalability of DIFF-FLOOD in larger settings are a significant contribution. The comparison of the number of parameters needed in different models to reach similar resolutions is revealing.
    *   **Practical application focus:** The discussion of scenario-based queries and the provision of an algorithm for answering them highlight the practical applicability of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset limitations:** While the TideWatch dataset is valuable, the paper acknowledges the lack of other publicly available inundation datasets.  This could limit the generalizability of the results to other coastal regions with different characteristics.  Testing on data with different underlying dynamics (e.g., different types of storm surges, varying geological features) is needed to fully validate the robustness of DIFF-FLOOD.
    *   **Lack of detailed discussion of model complexity:** While scalability is addressed, there isn't an in-depth discussion of the computational cost (training time, inference time) of DIFF-FLOOD compared to simpler methods. What are the trade-offs in using this model at different levels of precision?
    *   **Overly complex baselines**: The authors rightly point out that some methods cannot scale for larger scenarios (DiffSTG), so these fail, rendering them less informative in the experiments overall and perhaps inflating the relative improvements of DIFF-FLOOD. Were there simpler non-DL baselines used that might offer tradeoffs in speed?
    *   **Limited discussion of uncertainty:** The paper emphasizes probabilistic forecasting but doesn't delve deeply into the characterization and calibration of the uncertainty estimates produced by the model.  Understanding the reliability of the uncertainty predictions is crucial for real-world applications.
    *  **Inference Method**: Algorithm 2 needs more fleshing out. How do the authors chose a number of noise steps?
    *   **Limited analysis of error patterns:** While aggregate performance metrics are provided, there is little discussion of *where* the model makes mistakes. Are there specific geographic regions or types of events where it performs poorly? An analysis of error patterns would provide valuable insights for further model refinement and practical application.

*   **Potential Influence:** DIFF-FLOOD has the potential to influence research in coastal inundation forecasting by demonstrating the effectiveness of denoising diffusion models for this task.  It could also inspire further work on developing scalable spatiotemporal forecasting methods for other environmental applications. The focus on answering policy-relevant queries could encourage the development of similar decision-support tools. However, its influence will depend on whether other research groups can reproduce and build upon the results presented in this paper.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7/10 is appropriate. The paper presents a novel approach with promising results and demonstrates its potential for real-world impact. The scalability of the DIFF-FLOOD model is a strong point, as are the thorough experimental evaluations and targeted, policy-relevant outcomes. The application of the modeling to the city of Virginia Beach is also a nice demonstration. The weaknesses lie primarily in dataset limitations, limited discussions of the underlying error conditions and the omission of comparisons that trade off simplicity for performance.

### PillarMamba: Learning Local-Global Context for Roadside Point Cloud via Hybrid State Space Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05397v1)
- **Authors**: Zhang Zhang, Chao Sun, Chao Yue, Da Wen, Tianze Wang, Jianghao Leng
- **Abstract**: Serving the Intelligent Transport System (ITS) and Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X) tasks, roadside perception has received increasing attention in recent years, as it can extend the perception range of connected vehicles and improve traffic safety. However, roadside point cloud oriented 3D object detection has not been effectively explored.
